# PROJECT MASTER PLAN — Offline Multi-Agent Decision Transformer for Traffic Signal Control

**Version:** 1.6 · **Last updated:** 2026-08-24 · **Maintained in:** Master Coordination Chat
**Mentor:** Paweł Gora (Quantum AI Foundation) · **Target:** arXiv → IEEE ITSC / IEEE T-ITS / TRB (Q2/Q1)

---

## 1. Headline Contribution (FROZEN — do not renegotiate in sub-chats)

> **"Offline Multi-Agent Decision Transformer (MADT) for traffic signal control, trained purely from logged trajectories, evaluated on a rigorous multi-simulator platform — with cross-backend transfer (CityFlow → SUMO) and rare-event robustness as the headline evaluation axes."**

Three claims, each independently defensible (hardened 2026-07-10 after external critique review):

| # | Claim | Why it survives review |
|---|-------|------------------------|
| C1 | **Data-quality study:** MADT performance as a function of a D4RL-style dataset ladder (random / fixed-time / max-pressure / medium-MAPPO / expert-MAPPO / mixed), trained with **no online exploration** from logged operational data. Ladder released publicly. | Fixes the circularity trap (expert data → trivial imitation; weak data → probably fails). Positioned vs DTLight's DTRL (2 policies, SUMO-only, independent agents): ours has 6 tiers, multi-agent local rewards, **dual-backend paired**. "No online exploration" replaces the rhetorically weak "zero simulator interaction." **Load-bearing.** |
| C2 | **Pre-registered RQ:** does offline sequence modeling degrade more gracefully under scenario shift (demand shocks, incidents, sensor dropout)? Answered via a **2×2 design** ({nominal, shift-augmented} training data × {MADT, MAPPO}) + mechanism ablations (context length, data diversity). | The 2×2 with domain-randomized MAPPO removes the data/architecture confound (the single most likely rejection reason). Known DT risk under shift (ESPER/infeasible-RTG) is addressed by calibrated prompting — and plausibly explains DataLight's negative result. Negative/mixed answer still publishes. **Load-bearing.** |
| C3 | **Dynamics-shift study:** transfer *curve* of CityFlow-trained MADT on SUMO — zero-shot → few-shot (k ∈ {5, 20, 100} episodes, collected by a MaxPressure probe, spanning scenario variants) → full retrain — with within-backend-normalized metrics (vs fixed-time AND max-pressure anchors) and calibrated RTG prompting. Interface documented precisely; one alternative-state-encoding robustness ablation. | 🚨 **CORRECTED 2026-08-17 — THE NON-EXISTENCE CLAIM THAT STOOD HERE IS WITHDRAWN. It read: *"No systematic cross-simulator dynamics-shift study for TSC exists"*, and it is FALSE.** CityFlow→SUMO is an established paradigm: **UGAT** (Da et al., CDC'23) demonstrates and mitigates the TSC sim-to-real gap; **PromptGAT** (arXiv 2308.14284, AAAI'24) does it with LLM-based dynamics profiling over four scenarios; **JL-GAT** (arXiv 2507.15174, 2025) extends it multi-agent; and **LibSignal** (Mei et al., MLJ 2024) exists to provide cross-simulator comparison with SUMO↔CityFlow conversion described as *carefully calibrated*. ⚠️ **None of these names appeared anywhere in this plan before today** (`LibSignal` 0, `PromptGAT` 0, `UGAT` 0, `JL-GAT` 0, `grounded action` 0). **C3's positioning may NOT rest on the axis being unoccupied.** ⚠️ **SHARPENED 2026-08-17, ONE DAY AFTER THE ABOVE, BY THE USER READING PromptGAT (2308.14284v6) AND LibSignal (2211.10649v2) IN FULL — and it corrects MY correction, which was itself overstated.** I had written *"nobody measures a transfer curve for an offline sequence model"*; **the zero-shot half of that is FALSE.** **PromptGAT's Table 2 reports Direct-Transfer rows — an explicitly FROZEN DQN policy applied WITHOUT adaptation — as a result, not as motivation** (V1 ATT 158.93, gap 47.69; V4 ATT 332.48, gap 221.25). **So *"nobody measures zero-shot cross-simulator transfer for TSC"* must never be written.**
⭐ **WHAT SURVIVES IS NARROWER, AND IT RESTS ON TWO THINGS IN THEIR OWN SOURCES.** **(i) PromptGAT's target is SUMO WITH DELIBERATELY ALTERED VEHICLE PARAMETERS** — their Table 1 defines V0 as default and V1–V4 as modified accel/decel/eDecel/sDelay, and **Table 2 reports ONLY V1–V4. V0, the pure engine-versus-engine comparison at default parameters, appears nowhere in their results**, so their gap **conflates engine difference with intentional parameter shift and never isolates the first.** ⚠️ **STRENGTHENED 2026-08-17 — the earlier form of this point was too weak and is replaced.** *(Date corrected 2026-08-18 from "2026-08-18": `git blame` puts this line in commit `4ad5778`, authored 2026-08-17 22:33, so the label postdated its own commit — the sixth site of the class swept that day.)* It said they *"forgot V0"*. **Our own `docs/notes/P7.0_vtype_investigation.md` shows V0 WOULD NOT HAVE BEEN PARITY EITHER:** `DEFAULT_VEHTYPE` runs `tau = 1.0` against CityFlow's `headwayTime = 2.0`, and the shipped `pkw` **sets no `tau` at all** (note line 41). **So the correct claim is not that they omitted a comparison, but that EVEN DEFAULT-VS-DEFAULT IS CONFOUNDED — AND WE MEASURED BY HOW MUCH: `tau` alone moves ATT 276.45 → 415.04 and arrivals 1578 → 1201.** **That is stronger, fairer to them, and not rebuttable.** ⚠️ **One precision from the same note, because it bounds our own claim: hangzhou's lane speeds are 11.11 / 9.84 / 9.26 / 3.65 m/s, all at or below CityFlow's 11.11, and SUMO takes `min(vType.maxSpeed, laneSpeed × speedFactor)` — so the 55.556 ceiling is MASKED on this network, which is why binding `pkw` alone was only +0.6 %. `tau` is the one that bites HERE; on a network with higher lane limits `maxSpeed` would bite too, so the confound's SIZE is network-dependent and must not be quoted as a constant.** **P7.0 did the opposite: `cf_parity` with `tau = 2.0` bound, measuring the engine difference with vehicle dynamics HELD EQUAL** — and we measured `tau` at **+49 % travel time**, so that is not a detail. **(ii) LibSignal DISCLAIMS transfer in its own words** — *"a possible testbed for Sim-to-Real transfer, which is not covered by this paper"* — and **its Appendix A.5 calibration touches routing, virtual nodes, signal intervals, dynamic routing and departure ordering, and NOT accel, decel, tau or maxSpeed.** Their Table 3 also shows the post-calibration gap is **policy-dependent** (Grid4x4 MaxPressure 142.53 vs 143.83, FixedTime 179.66 vs 194.65; HangZhou4x4 FixedTime 689.02 vs 535.01) — **consistent with P7.0's Branch C rather than in tension with it.**
⭐ **(iii) A THIRD CONFOUND NOBODY NAMED: CONVERSION QUALITY.** **LibSignal's Table 3, Cologne1x1, SOTL on the CityFlow side: 1356.6037 before calibration, 50.5813 after — TWENTY-SEVEN-FOLD.** No controller produces 1356 s at a single intersection on a correct network; **that is a broken network conversion being repaired, not dynamics tuning.** **So the residual cross-backend gap in the literature conflates THREE things: engine semantics, vehicle-dynamics parameters, and CONVERTER ARTEFACTS.**
🚨 **AND THIS IS WHERE `P11.5`'s REFUSAL BECOMES OUR STRONGEST METHODOLOGICAL ARGUMENT, WHICH IT IS NOWHERE RECORDED AS.** P11.5 refuses converted pairs in the C3 curve and currently reads as **conservatism**. It is not: **our scenarios are NATIVELY AUTHORED in both backends, so the converter confound is ABSENT BY CONSTRUCTION — in a literature where it is present, unquantified, and demonstrably capable of a 27× artefact.** **Write it as an argument, not as a caution.**
> **THE CORRECTED C3 POSITIONING — roughly 3× narrower than §1 implied, and true.** Prior work measures the gap against a **CONFIGURED target domain** (PromptGAT's V1–V4 alter `accel`/`decel`/`eDecel`/`sDelay` **by design**), and the calibration work aligns **networks, routes and signal timing but not vehicle dynamics** (LibSignal A.5). **Nobody has measured the RESIDUAL ENGINE GAP UNDER DYNAMICS PARITY, on NATIVELY-AUTHORED rather than converted scenarios — and nobody has done it for an OFFLINE SEQUENCE MODEL.**
> **Three features a referee checks one at a time: PARITY (not deliberate shift) · NATIVE (not converted) · OFFLINE SEQUENCE MODEL (not online DQN). Smaller than what §1 implied. True beats large.**

**⚠️ CLAIM CONSTRAINT — THE RETURN PROMPT IS A WEAK LEVER IN-DOMAIN, MEASURED (2026-08-13, P4.3).**
Across a **pre-declared nine-point grid spanning 13,000 units of return**, the DT's held-out ATT moves
by **0.9026** end to end, and the naive-to-best contrast is **0.3994** (paired, 100 shared draws,
n = 500 per point). **Only 3 of 8 adjacent steps resolve and none of the 5 interior ones do.** So:
**no claim may present in-domain return-prompt calibration as a substantial lever**, and the named
method component's case rests on the **cross-domain** setting — where the naive target is not merely
suboptimal but *meaningless*, because the return scale differs between backends. C3/P7 is where the
mechanism is tested; **P4.3 tested its components and found the in-domain headroom small.**
⚠️ **Two further constraints from the same run.** (i) **The best point sits on the grid's boundary**
(`target = 0`, the most optimistic point declared), so the optimum may lie outside it; **the grid was
deliberately not extended, because extending a declared grid because its edge won is selection on the
result.** (ii) **P4's `4.4 ATT` single-episode figure overstated the effect 11×.** Any future use of a
one-episode probe as a magnitude carries that ratio as its prior.

**⚠️ CLAIM CONSTRAINT — C1's CONCEPT IS 2021 PRIOR ART IN ANOTHER DOMAIN (verified 2026-08-12).**
`arXiv:2112.02845` (Meng et al., peer-reviewed in *Machine Intelligence Research*) introduces **"the
first offline MARL dataset with diverse quality levels"** on StarCraft II, proposes **MADT**, and
studies **offline pre-training → online fine-tuning with few-shot and zero-shot transfer**. That is
C1's ladder concept **and** C3's transfer-curve concept, as one package, five years before us.
**C1 survives; the sentence "we introduce the study of offline MARL performance as a function of data
quality" does not, and must never be written.** What survives is the **TSC instantiation**: 6 tiers
against their 3, multi-agent *local* rewards, dual-backend paired scenarios, the method × tier grid
(P4.6), F3's selection-mechanism decomposition, and the pre-registration. §1's existing wording is
already hedged this way ("ours has 6 tiers, multi-agent local rewards, dual-backend paired") and is
correct as it stands. **Binding: 2112.02845 is cited where C1 is INTRODUCED, not only in the prior-art
list** — a referee who knows it will look for it there first.

**⚠️ CLAIM CONSTRAINT — action masking (measured 2026-08-07, before the claim was written).** The
corpus is collected under `control_mode: acyclic`, whose `available_actions()` returns **every** green
phase, so **`avail_mask` never binds anywhere in our data: 460/460 per-intersection streams are
entirely `True`.** This is binding on what C1/C2/C3 may say. Masking stays in the architecture — the
env raises on illegal actions, masks bind under cyclic and RESCO-cyclic modes, and P6's lane closures
are designed to make them bind — but on **this** corpus a no-mask ablation measures nothing, and no
paper sentence may credit masked-action modelling as a learned capability or as a source of our
performance. Making masking a *result* requires a corpus collected under a mode where it binds: a new
collection, not a re-analysis. Found by the P3 implementer in pre-flight, which is the only cheap time
to find it — after the claim is drafted it costs a retraction.

**Named method component:** *probe-calibrated return prompting* — target RTG expressed relative to a cheap
in-domain probe policy (MaxPressure) return distribution (e.g., quantile-space). One mechanism serving
both C2 (infeasible-RTG under scenario shift) and C3 (reward-scale shift across backends), with its own
ablation. ⚠️ **NOVELTY NARROWED 2026-08-01:** DTLight already sets RTG as `max_offline_return × scale`
(γ_eval = 0.2, γ_online = 0.3) *with its own ablation figure*, explicitly to avoid hand-setting RTG per
agent. So "scale-factor RTG conditioning **within** a domain is taken." Our defensible remainder: RTG
calibrated **in the target domain** from a probe policy run there, for the cross-backend case DTLight
never faces (it never transfers between simulators). Position precisely against their Fig. 4 or a
reviewer will call it a re-run.

**Controversy framing (the paper's hook) — SHARPENED 2026-08-01 after reading both primary sources:**
DataLight (2303.10828) states DT "cannot be applied for TSC", but its Appendix A.5 shows the negative
result rests on a configuration that is not a fair test of sequence modeling: **context length K ∈ {1, 2}
only** (K=1 is *no* sequence modeling), and **a single hardcoded RTG of −351** for all agents and
scenarios. Their DT scores 779–1295 s ATT against ~240 s for good methods, and "DT performs better
under K=1 than K=2" — consistent with a model that never had enough context to model anything.
DTLight (2312.07795) with a proper DT (GPT-2, 6 layers/8 heads teacher) beats all online baselines on
5–6 of 8 scenarios **purely offline**. So the contradiction is real but *explicable*, and our two
mechanisms are exactly the two DataLight omitted: context length (P5.3 ablation) and calibrated RTG
(P4.3). **Be scrupulously fair in the paper**: state that DataLight reported the failure under the
configurations it tested, and present our K-sweep as the test of their stated hypothesis, not as a
refutation of their integrity. DataLight's own diagnosis — "offline datasets mixed with trajectories
from multiple agents may make DT agents confused" — is a hypothesis our per-intersection sequence
design directly tests.

**Non-negotiable baselines (offline-RL venue standard):** BC and %BC on the expert slice (if BC matches MADT, sequence modeling adds nothing — must be tested), IQL (independent per-intersection), CQL optional; plus online MAPPO/IPPO/DQN, MaxPressure, fixed-time, random; plus **domain-randomized MAPPO** for C2.

## 1b. EXPLANATORY READING OF THE C1 GRID — what we FOUND (added 2026-08-14, after P4.6 merged)

⚠️ **This section is a different kind of object from §1's four claim constraints, and the distinction is
the point.** Those are **limits on what we may SAY**. These are **explanations of what we FOUND**, and
until now they existed **only in conversation** — the paper's central reading, unrecorded, while every
guard around it was written down. *(Grepped `main` before writing: "stitch" appeared exactly twice —
`PREREGISTRATION` H1 from 10 July, and `docs/plans/p4.md`.)*

🚨 **ALL FOUR ARE POST-HOC. P1, P2 and P3 ALL FAILED**, so every reading below was arrived at **after
seeing the data.** They are **legitimate as explanation and illegitimate as confirmation**, and the
paper must carry that label. **Each names the measurement it rests on AND the ones it does not.**
All figures below are from the **corrected** artifact, never the withdrawn void row.

**R1 — Cloning transfers quality and essentially nothing else.** BC lands within **1.51 ATT** of its own
behaviour policy on four tiers (`mappo1000` −0.4186 · `mappo500` −0.8136 · `random` −0.9296 ·
`maxpressure` −1.5019) and **+3.7616** on `fixedtime`. *Rests on:* the 20 BC cells against the five
held-out behaviour cells. *Does NOT test:* that nothing **but** quality transfers — P4.5's 92 % figure
was measured on **one** tier, and this extends it by pattern, not by measurement.

🚨 **SCOPE CONDITION ADDED 2026-08-18, AND IT APPLIES TO BOTH R2 AND R6: EVERYTHING BELOW WAS MEASURED ON hz1x1, WHERE A SINGLE INTERSECTION MAKES RETURNS TRIVIALLY COMPARABLE — SO THE CONDITION WAS INVISIBLE.** P5.1 ran the same unmodified `top_return_streams` on grid4x4's **16** intersections and `bc_top10` collapsed to **749.58**, with **505 of ~1300 vehicles still in network**. ⚠️ **The mechanism, stated at the strength the evidence supports — the phrase *"exact set identity"* stood here and is WITHDRAWN as an overstatement (P5.1's reviewer, m3): 302 of 320 kept streams are strictly above the cut and come from the 8 intersections with the highest own-max return, which are the QUIETEST — but 32 streams TIE at the cut (−114.0) and fill the last 18 slots, one of them belonging to B0, the 9th node. A reverse tie-break yields 9 intersections.** **302/320 from 8 nodes is overwhelming and the load-sorter mechanism is untouched; the set-identity phrasing was the coordinator's and does not survive** (C2 tops out at −64, D1 at −142). **Per-intersection returns on a network are dominated by LOAD, not by control quality, so a global return quantile is a LOAD SORTER — it trained on the eight quietest nodes and was asked to control sixteen.**
> **The transferable form: a return-quantile filter is a QUALITY filter only when returns are COMPARABLE ACROSS THE UNITS BEING FILTERED.** R2 and R6 were never wrong; **they were measured under a condition nobody could see was a condition**, and they may not be stated without it.
⚠️ **This does NOT retro-apply to P4.4–P4.7's merged cells** — on one intersection the filter did what those tasks reported. **Per-intersection %BC is a NEW ARM, declared and reported beside the global filter, never a re-specification of a method after watching it collapse** (§7's asymmetry). **The un-re-specified collapse IS the transferable result.**

**R2 — %BC CHANGES SIGN against its own behaviour policy.** Better on `maxpressure` **−11.1144**,
`mappo500` **−3.3396**, `mappo1000` **−2.4193**; **worse** on `fixedtime` **+13.9285** and `random`
**+3.3013**. ⚠️ **Two tiers, not one.** Reading: the top-decile filter selects the best behaviour **mode
available**, and on a uniformly weak single-controller tier there is no mode, so it selects noise —
**P4.5's three-instantiation hypothesis reaching its third instantiation.** *Rests on:* the sign change
itself, plus P4.5's F3 (`{202:10, 101:9, 505:1, 303:0, 404:0}`, r = −0.991). ⚠️ *Does NOT test that it
selects **noise*** — **P3 was the diagnostic for exactly that and it FAILED on `random`** (overlap 3/20,
p = 0.3213). **The "what does the filter select on weak data" question is OPEN, and R2 must not be
written as if P3 answered it.**
🚨 **NOR DID P4.7'S MIXTURES ANSWER IT, and that was a DESIGN ERROR OF MINE (2026-08-15, `DEFERRED` 46).**
`BRIEF_19`'s Q1 was **unfalsifiable by construction** — `mappo1000` spans **[−9991, −5762]** and
`random` spans **[−40294, −38369]**, a **+28,378 gap with 0 of 400 random streams above the expert
minimum** — so an all-expert top decile was certain before a single gradient step. **Caught by the
implementer, verified independently by me.** It is §7's 2026-08-07 rule (*a fixture that satisfies the
assertion by construction is a tautology*) recurring **at the level of a registered prediction**.
**What the mixtures DID show is A5's second axis: the kept decile concentrates on 3–4 of 5 expert
seeds — CHECKPOINT selection, P4.5's effect, not composition selection.** ⚠️ **Binding: no paper
sentence may say the mixture settled what the filter selects.** The design that would settle it is
measured in `DEFERRED` 46 — **`mappo1000 + mappo200`, 43.5 % overlap**, where a return filter must
discriminate and can fail.

### 🚨 PRE-REGISTERED BEFORE P5.1'S CAMPAIGN LANDS (2026-08-17, ~5 h before the artifacts exist)

**Everything here is recorded while `CAMPAIGN_COMPLETE` does not exist and `output/p5_1/` holds one
JSON. After the numbers land, any of it would be post-hoc.** All four quotations below were read
**first-hand by the user in the PDFs**, not relayed.

**C1 — A NULL ON `dt_spatial` vs `dt_nomix` IS NOT SELF-INTERPRETING. IT IS ONE PAPER'S PUBLISHED
PREDICTION AT EXACTLY OUR NETWORK SIZE.**
**arXiv:2603.22315** — already filed in §1 and P6.1 as an EV-corridor paper — **ablates graph attention
by grid size in its Table 9**: *"Removing GAT has minimal effect on 4x4 (91.1 s vs. 92.4 s, within
noise) but causes large degradation on 6x6 (+9.5%, 162.8 s vs. 148.7 s) and 8x8 (+14.1%, 258.1 s vs.
226.3 s)."* Its C=30 variant on 4x4 reaches **89.8 s against single-agent DT's 88.6 s** — *"nearly
matches"*. **arXiv:2602.02903 reports the OPPOSITE at the same size**: *"on Grid 4x4, the gap widens to
9.2% … Without graph attention, MADT performs similarly to IndependentDT (356.1s vs. 356.2s),
confirming that the graph attention mechanism is the primary driver of coordination benefits."*
⚠️ **Caveats, so this is not read as stronger than it is: different simulators, different tasks
(EV-corridor ETT vs network ATT), different context lengths, absolute numbers not comparable to our
anchors, both v1 preprints. What transfers is the DIRECTION and rough MAGNITUDE of the −GAT contrast,
weakly. The topology does match — 4×4, 16 nodes.**
> **BINDING: a null may NOT be written as *"the last live explanation of the DT deficit is excluded"*.**
> That would report **untested at a size where our instrument has no measured resolution** as **tested
> and cleared**. **The honest sentence for a null is: *"no detectable effect at 16 intersections, where
> the literature disagrees"*, with whatever power we can compute.**

**C2 — THE FRAMING THAT SURVIVES EITHER OUTCOME, AND IT IS STRONGER THAN THE ONE IT REPLACES.**
P5.1 is currently positioned as a claim about **us** — the last explanation of our deficit. **It is
better positioned as a THIRD, PRE-REGISTERED measurement of a contrast on which two 2026 preprints
DISAGREE, on a different engine, with 5 seeds and paired tests, which neither of them has.**
**That publishes under either outcome, which is what `PREREGISTRATION` §10 already asks of us.**

**C3 — THE HEAD-COUNT CONFOUND, MEASURED BY THE COORDINATOR IN THE WORKTREE BEFORE THE CAMPAIGN
LANDED.** `agent/SpatialDTAgent.py:136` declares **`n_head: int = 1`**, inherited from
`agent/DTAgent.py:107` — **and `:233-235` shows the TEMPORAL and SPATIAL sublayers share it, so the
GAT-equivalent is ALSO single-head.** Both in-domain papers use **4 heads with 2 GAT layers**
(2603.22315: *"d=128, L=3, NH=4, 2 GAT layers with 4 heads"*; 2602.02903 likewise).
> **BINDING: a collapse or a null is CONFOUNDED with head count against both architectures ours is
> closest to, and the qualifier is registered here BEFORE the result.** ⚠️ **`DEFERRED` 52's defence —
> that this is Chen et al. 2021's published configuration — is true about PROVENANCE and weaker about
> DOMAIN FIT, since both in-domain papers chose 4.** No sweep tonight; the qualifier is what had to be
> pre-result.

**C4 — §1's PROMPT SENTENCE MUST NOT BE READABLE AS EXCLUDING CORPUS-SIDE RETURN SPREAD.**
P4.3 measured the prompt at **0.9026 ATT** across a 13,000-wide grid, and §1 reads that as measured and
excluded. **An inference-time sweep cannot distinguish two readings: (i) the model is robust to the
target, so the prompt is not the mechanism; (ii) the model learned to IGNORE the RTG token because our
corpus lacks the return spread for it to be identifiable.** ⚠️ **Under (ii), inertness IS the mechanism
rather than its exclusion — and it explains R1 in one line, because a DT whose RTG token carries
nothing is BC with extra parameters, which is exactly what BC-within-1.51-ATT looks like.**
For scale, 2603.22315's Table 8 sweeps composition on 4×4 — expert-only **118.5** ETT against their
70/15/15 mixture's **88.6**, i.e. **expert-only is 33.7 % WORSE** — and 2602.02903 measures removing
return conditioning at **5.2 %** (328.5 → 345.6).
> **BINDING: the prompt is a weak lever IN THIS CORPUS. The sentence may not be read as excluding
> corpus-side return spread, which was NEVER MEASURED. The discriminating measurement is the no-RTG
> ablation, already registered as P5.3 and named in `docs/plans/p4.4.md:295`.**

### ⭐⭐ THE MOST IMPORTANT THING P4.7 PRODUCED IS THE FALSIFICATION OF R3 — AND R3 WAS MINE

**Stated as the headline rather than buried as an amendment, because the episode is the argument for
the whole apparatus.** R3 was **the coordinator's reading**, drawn from the single-controller ladder —
the axis §1's constraint (a) explicitly says **confounds quality with state coverage** — and it was
recorded **post-hoc-labelled at the user's insistence**. **The composition axis existed precisely to
separate those two things, and the first thing it did was kill a reading taken from the axis that
conflates them.**

> **Had R3 gone into the paper as a FINDING rather than as a labelled READING, it would be false
> today.** That is the post-hoc labelling discipline paying for itself in a measurable way, on a
> specific sentence, within one task of being written.

⚠️ **And the replacement is better than what it replaces, which is the part worth carrying:**
*"how weak AND how unimodal"* names a **mechanism** — a bimodal corpus contains good trajectories to
clone, so %BC simply takes them — where *"how weak"* named only a **correlation**. **It is testable
rather than descriptive, and `DEFERRED` 46's overlapping-component design is what would test it**,
which is a second reason that row is worth more than it looked when parked.

🚨 **R3 IS AMENDED BY P4.7 (2026-08-15) — THE AXIS IS NOT "HOW WEAK" BUT "HOW WEAK *AND UNIMODAL*".**
**Measured by me from the raw records:** IQL ranks **1 of 4 on all three weak SINGLE-CONTROLLER tiers**
(`maxpressure`, `fixedtime`, `random`) and **3 of 4 on all three MIXTURES** — and a mixture is **weak on
average** (constructed references **322.24 / 267.27 / 212.27**, i.e. worse than `maxpressure`'s 176.89)
**but BIMODAL.** So *"IQL improves most where the data is worst"* **does not survive the
quality-versus-composition distinction**: on a bimodal corpus there are good trajectories to clone, and
%BC simply takes them. ⭐ **This is the payoff of §1's constraint (a) — the composition axis was built
to separate quality from coverage, and the first thing it did was falsify a reading drawn from the
single-controller ladder alone.** ⚠️ *Consistent with, and NOT tested by,* the stitching explanation:
composing fragments helps when there is nothing good to clone, and loses to cloning when there is.
**R3's original text stands below as what was measured on the single-controller axis.**
✅ **AND THE FALSIFICATION DOES NOT DEPEND ON THE CONSTRUCTED REFERENCE — checked, because it would
otherwise rest on the one quantity in P4.7 that is neither measured nor re-used from a merged
artifact.** **R3's falsification survives on THREE independent operationalisations of "how weak the data is"** —
the third supplied by P4.7's reviewer, using neither the construction nor BC:

1. **The constructed references** — `322.24 / 267.27 / 212.27`, all worse than `maxpressure`'s 176.89.
2. **The BC proxy (the coordinator's).** BC clones its data and does nothing else, so its own cell mean
   measures how cloneable a corpus is: `bc@mix33` **261.2149** against `bc@fixedtime` **265.8322**, yet
   IQL ranks **3/4** on the first and **1/4** on the second. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-15 by the reviewer,
   and the correction is the amendment's own thesis: BC is a sound proxy only for the MEAN.** This line
   read *"equally cloneable to within 4.6173 ATT"*; it must read **equally cloneable ON AVERAGE, and
   radically differently PER EPISODE** — `bc@mix33` has sd **158.06** with **51 %** of episodes below
   150 ATT, `bc@fixedtime` sd **8.91** with **0 %** below 150. **The very bimodality that makes the two
   corpora equally cloneable on average is what makes IQL's rank invert.**
3. **Mean training-set return, read from the committed declarations** (the reviewer's, no construction
   and no BC): `fixedtime` **−31,328.1** (IQL 1/4) against `mix33` **−28,833.3** (IQL 3/4), and
   `maxpressure` **−18,546.5** (1/4) against `mix67` **−17,735.3** (3/4) — **matched to 8 % and 5 % on
   corpus quality, ranks inverted**, with the ranges telling the story
   (`fixedtime [−33225, −29707]` unimodal against `mix33 [−40223, −5994]` bimodal).

**No constructed quantity enters (2) or (3), so the falsification does not rest on the one number in
P4.7 that is neither measured nor re-used from a merged artifact.**

**R3 — IQL improves most where the data is worst, AT THE ENDPOINTS ONLY.** IQL over BC, tiers ordered
**worst data first**: `random` **+34.5561** · `fixedtime` **+5.4937** · `maxpressure` **+16.5092** ·
`mappo500` **+2.4585** · `mappo1000` **+1.6871**. 🚨 **THIS IS NOT MONOTONE — `fixedtime` breaks it**,
sitting below `maxpressure` despite being worse data. **Report it by its endpoints (34.56 against
1.69), never as a trend**, exactly as P4.3's landscape is reported. *Rests on:* five paired
differences. ⚠️ *Does NOT test the STITCHING explanation* — that expectile regression over states,
rather than a max over actions, lets IQL compose a policy from fragments of poor trajectories while
BC and %BC clone whole ones. **That reading is CONSISTENT WITH the pattern and NOT TESTED BY it.**
Testing it needs a **trajectory-fragment diagnostic that nobody has run**, and `fixedtime`'s
non-monotonicity is the first thing such a diagnostic would have to explain.

**R4 — a CROSSOVER with the inversion point BRACKETED TO ONE RUNG, and that rung is a tie.** %BC vs IQL,
paired: `mappo1000` **−0.3137**, CI [−0.5680, −0.0594] **resolves, %BC leads** · `mappo500` **−0.0675**,
CI [−0.3169, +0.1819] **DOES NOT RESOLVE** · `maxpressure` **+6.8966** · `fixedtime` **+15.6606** ·
`random` **+38.7869**, all three resolving, **IQL leads**. **So the crossing is located to within one
rung and that rung is a statistical tie** — which is a stronger and more honest statement than naming
a point. *Does NOT test:* that the inversion is caused by data quality rather than by anything else
covarying with tier identity — **§1 already records that our ladder confounds quality with state
coverage**, and P4.7's mixture tiers are the design that separates them.

**R6 — %BC IS ALMOST INDIFFERENT TO DILUTION, AND THIS IS THE MOST PRACTICALLY USEFUL NUMBER IN C1 (added 2026-08-15 from P4.7, verified by me).**
`bc_top10@mix33` = **104.3372** against `bc_top10@mappo1000` = **103.1627**. **Replacing 134 of 200
expert streams with random ones costs 1.1745 ATT; at mix67 the cost is 0.0734.** ⚠️ **Stated as what it
is: on THIS corpus, whose expert and random components do not overlap at all (`DEFERRED` 46), a
top-decile filter recovers the expert fraction essentially for free.** *Does NOT test* that the same
holds when components overlap — which is exactly what `DEFERRED` 46's `mappo1000+mappo200` design is
for. **The practical reading — you can dilute an expert corpus threefold and a return filter barely
notices — is worth reporting, and its scope condition must travel with it.**

⚠️ **R7 IS A DESCRIPTIVE COUNT, MUST BE STATED AS ONE, AND ITS POPULATION IS EIGHT `cf_hz1x1` TIERS — see the scope block under R7 itself, added later the same day after P5.1's ninth tier was read (added 2026-08-18). Measured today: `p4_6_grid.json`'s `multiplicity` key is NOT a correction — it reads *"70 paired comparisons, no correction applied and none needed: no decision rule is attached to any of them"*, and `p4_4_baselines.json` and `p4_gate.json` carry no such key at all.** **That declaration is legitimate for the comparisons — and *"the DT leads zero of eight tiers"* ATTACHES the decision rule it says is absent**, over **32 cells and 90 paired comparisons.**
> **Ruled: R7 is a COUNT OF ORDERINGS, not an inferential claim, and the paper states it that way — *"the DT does not lead on any of the eight tiers we measured"*.** That is **free, true today, and unbuyable**; it is barely weaker rhetorically and it removes the family-wise question rather than answering it badly. ⚠️ **To say anything STRONGER — that the DT does not lead in general — needs a family-wise correction that does not exist, and P8.1 has not run.** **Neither R7 nor any sentence derived from it may carry inferential weight until it does.**

🚨 **ATT IS BOUNDED BELOW AND RIGHT-SKEWED, AND `mean ± CI95` IS A QUESTIONABLE SUMMARY OF IT (added 2026-08-18).**
**Our own sharpest instance: `bc_top10@grid4x4` at 749.58 with 505 of ~1300 vehicles still in network — a mean over episodes where control PARTIALLY FAILED, reported as though it were a central tendency.** P5.1's m7 makes the same point for `dt_spatial` (vehicles at horizon **16.2 / 133.9 / 123.2 / 75.7 / 49.4** against a control at ~15). Earlier instances were already on record and unread as a class: **`bc@mix50` ran 135.6 → 285.4 across seeds**, and **`bc@mix33` had sd 158.06 with 51 % of episodes below 150 ATT.**
> **The registered paired protocol is NOT changed — it works and it is registered.** **What is required is that the paper STATES WHY THE MEAN IS THE PRIMARY**, and reports **medians, bootstrap intervals or a distributional comparison beside it for every arm whose distribution is visibly not unimodal.** ⚠️ ***"We used the mean because it was registered"* is an answer about our process, not about the quantity** — and a reviewer with a statistics background asks this on the first pass.

**R7 — the DT's best relative standing anywhere is on the mixtures, and it changes nothing (2026-08-15).**
**2 of 4 on all three**, its best rank in the study — and it still **leads on ZERO of eight tiers**.
§1's claim constraint is unchanged and now rests on **eight** tiers rather than five.
🚨 **SCOPE ADDED 2026-08-18, AND IT IS THE SAME CLASS THIS PROJECT KEEPS CATCHING — A COUNT TRUE OF A
SAMPLE, READ AS A PROPERTY OF THE POPULATION. ALL EIGHT TIERS ARE `cf_hz1x1`, A SINGLE INTERSECTION.**
**The ninth tier — `cf_grid4x4@mappo1000`, 16 intersections — is LED by a DT arm** (`dt_nomix`, 1 of 5,
beating its behaviour policy; §1's 2026-08-18 update carries the four limits). **The correct sentence
is *"the DT leads none of the eight single-intersection tiers we measured"*, and it may not be written
as *"the DT does not lead"*.** ⚠️ **R7's descriptive-count rule does not repair this**: a count with the
wrong population is wrong before any inference is attempted.

### ⭐ R5 — THE ONE OBSERVATION REGISTERED IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION, AND THEREFORE THE STRONGEST

**`BRIEF_11` §7 bound the interpretation BEFORE P4.4 ran:** *"a LOSING untuned IQL cannot support any
claim of MADT superiority."* **We wrote that guard because we expected IQL to lose. It won — and it
now wins the entire weak half of the ladder.**

**Verified in source today** (`offline/offline_baselines.py:197-200, 1594-1603, 1935-1936`): IQL runs
`tau = 0.7`, `beta = 3.0`, `gamma = 0.99`, Polyak `0.005`, weight clip `100`, and a
`1000/(max_return − min_return)` reward scale — **published D4RL LOCOMOTION values, i.e. continuous-control
MuJoCo settings transplanted onto a discrete 8-phase action space, with a normalisation convention used
outside the domain it was defined for. NOT ONE HAS EVER BEEN TUNED FOR THIS PROBLEM**, and the module's
own docstring says so.

> **Binding, and it must be stated in the direction that cuts against us:** a method configured
> **against itself** beats every other arm on three of five tiers. **A tuned IQL would presumably do
> better, so the measured gap is a LOWER bound on IQL's advantage and an UPPER bound on the DT's
> standing against it.** State that explicitly — a referee will ask otherwise.

**It also retires an objection we would otherwise have to answer: *"your IQL was a straw man."* It
cannot be.** It lost every configuration argument in advance and won anyway. **The registration written
to guard against a weak baseline ended up certifying a strong one** — and until this section existed,
the record held the guard without the outcome.

---

**Unified narrative:** *Offline MADT for TSC under two axes of distribution shift — scenario shift (C2) and dynamics shift (C3) — with the data-quality ladder (C1) as the foundation.* Enabled by the repo's **6** genuinely paired scenarios (4× hangzhou_1x1 + cologne1 + cologne3 have flow.json AND .rou.xml AND .net.xml). **CORRECTION 2026-07-27: grid4x4 is NOT paired** — its `.sumocfg` references a `grid4x4.rou.xml` that does not exist in the repo, and there is no `.net.xml`. C3 transfer therefore runs on hangzhou_1x1 and cologne1/3; grid4x4 stays a CityFlow-only coordination scenario unless someone generates its SUMO files.

**Closest prior art (cite prominently or die):** ⚠️ **arXiv:2112.02845 — Meng et al., "Offline Pre-trained Multi-Agent Decision Transformer", model named MADT, PEER-REVIEWED in *Machine Intelligence Research* (s11633-022-1383-7). Our exact method class — offline + multi-agent + decision transformer — on SMAC rather than TSC. ADDED 2026-08-12: it was absent from this list and from every project document, which is a citation gap a referee finds in one search, and it is where the name "MADT" actually comes from.** · arXiv:2602.02903 (Su, Sun & Deng, 2 Feb 2026, *"Spatiotemporal Decision Transformer for Traffic Coordination"* — **verified 2026-08-12: it names its own model MADT, in our exact domain**; architecture overlap, no transfer/ladder); DTLight 2312.07795 + DTRL datasets; DataLight 2303.10828 (negative DT result — our hook); X-Light (cross-city transfer); OffLight; TransformerLight; arXiv:2603.22315 (offline DT for emergency-vehicle corridors, Mar 2026 — overlaps C2's EV perturbation; ours is a robustness study, theirs an EV method); iLLM-TSC; CoLLMLight; LATS. Space is moving monthly — speed on P1–P5 matters.

---

## 2. Hardware & Compute Budget

- **GPU:** NVIDIA RTX 5080 (ample VRAM for DT training; 7–8B local LLM in 4-bit for the optional teacher extension)
- **RAM:** 64 GB — sized for large offline trajectory corpora held in memory during training
- **CPU:** high-end multi-core — the actual bottleneck is simulation; data collection parallelizes across CPU workers (one env per process)
- **Strategy:** simulators are CPU-bound → generate the offline corpus in parallel overnight; DT training is pure supervised learning → GPU-efficient, fast iteration. This asymmetry is *the reason* the offline approach fits our hardware.

---

## 3. Baseline Platform (rltraffic) — verified facts

Repo: `github.com/RusieckiFilip/rltraffic` — the June 2026 UW bachelor's thesis of Bibrowski, Bublik, Pisula & Woliński (sup. G. Grudziński), lineage RESCO → RESCO TensorCell → this framework. Verified by direct code inspection 2026-07-08; thesis PDF reviewed 2026-07-09. Thesis-documented facts: ~3× training speedup vs TensorCell baseline (libsumo in-process + metric caching; ~200k simulator calls/episode on cologne3, only ~2% advance the clock); backend qualitative consistency SUMO↔CityFlow (smoothed-reward corr. 0.93) but **absolute metrics not interchangeable** (CityFlow jumpier, occasional gridlock episodes); IDQN(CNN) most sample-efficient on grid scenarios (spatial inductive bias, Fig. 5.9 ablation); IPPO/MAPPO unconverged at 200 eps on hangzhou_4x4 (Fig. 5.4); thesis experiments use the **PressLight** reward. Our paper cites this as the platform paper; ours is the method paper.

- **Three simulator backends, one API:** CityFlow (vendored, patched for Python 3.12+), SUMO (traci/libsumo), MOSS (GPU). Agents/rewards/metrics/states are backend-neutral.
- **Agents present:** DQN, DQNAgentPFRL (RESCO parity), IPPO, MAPPO (centralized critic + running normalization). Baselines: MaxPressure, random. *(Corrected 2026-08-05: this line previously read "fixed-time via phase-control modes" — false. The phase-control modes supply timing **semantics** (clearance, min/max green), never a controller that emits a schedule. No fixed-time controller existed until P2.5 built one.)*
- **Safety semantics:** 4 phase-control modes (acyclic, bounded, cyclic, RESCO-cyclic); enforced yellow/all-red clearance, min/max green; env raises on illegal actions.
- **Scenarios available:** hangzhou_1x1 (×4 variants), hangzhou_4x4_gudang, hangzhou_4x4_hetero, grid4x4, manhattan_28x7, cologne1, cologne3, aigen_1x1, bb5b.
- **Experiment harness:** JSON-config `env × agent × seed` matrix; paired-seed eval; outputs `results.json`, `summary.csv`, plots.
- **Gap we fill:** no offline-RL data path exists — no trajectory logger, no offline dataset format, no sequence-model agent. That is our contribution surface.

### 3.1 P0.2 Baseline anchors (2026-07-09, 3 seeds, 60 train episodes, avg travel time in s)

| Scenario | MaxPressure | Random | MAPPO@60ep |
|---|---|---|---|
| cf_hz1x1 | **160.56 ± 0.00** | 307.53 ± 0.60 | 197.91 ± 1.78 |
| cf_grid4x4 | **141.65 ± 0.00** | 207.26 ± 1.93 | 632.95 ± 51.63 (gridlock, 1040 veh stuck) |

🚨🚨 **NOT SETTLED (2026-08-07) — EVERY MAPPO-vs-MaxPressure ORDERING IN THIS PLAN IS PROVISIONAL, AND
TWO OF THEM ARE INSIDE THE ERROR BAR OF THE METRIC ITSELF.** P2.6 measured, on one real corpus episode
using the new v1.1 field, `att_horizon = 178.90` against `att_per_step.mean() = 127.88` — **a 40 %
gap between the registered metric and the legacy one, on our own data.** Every MAPPO-vs-MaxPressure
comparison we have is in `att_running_mean` (`run.py` prints what the frozen runner computes). The
margins being defended:

| comparison | hz1x1 | grid4x4 | cologne3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| MAPPO@1000 vs MP (2026-08-06 row) | MAPPO +43.6 % | MAPPO **+2.5 %** | MAPPO **+5.8 %** |
| MAPPO@500 vs MP (2026-08-06 row) | MAPPO +40.6 % | MAPPO **−8.5 %** | MAPPO **−12.0 %** |

**The flip condition is computable and small.** On cf_grid4x4 the measured horizon/running-mean ratio
is MaxPressure **1.19**, Random 1.28, MAPPO@60 **2.33** (2026-08-06 row) — a spread of 1.96× *between
policies on one scenario*. MAPPO@1000 leads by 2.5 % there, so **the ordering reverses the moment
MAPPO@1000's ratio exceeds ~1.22**, which is barely above MaxPressure's own and far below MAPPO@60's.
cologne3 reverses above a 5.8 % ratio difference. **hz1x1's 43.6 % margin would require a ratio gap
larger than any we have measured, so it is the most likely to survive — but "most likely" is not
settled either.**

**Binding precondition: none of these cells may enter a figure, a table or a claim until recomputed in
`att_horizon`.** ⚠️ **UPDATE 2026-08-07 — the randomised-draw recomputation is DONE and both findings reproduce with their signs intact** (see the Decisions Log row of that date): MAPPO@500 gives MaxPressure +15.1 % on grid4x4 and +17.4 % on cologne3, against the legacy −8.5 % and −12.0 %. **The metric-artifact hypothesis is now substantially disfavoured.** These cells stay NOT SETTLED because they are nominal draw-0 by definition and the recomputation was on draws 1-200, but the outstanding run is now a confirmation rather than an open question. In particular the finding *"the ladder's expert tier is MAPPO on hz1x1 and MaxPressure
on the other two"* is **suspended**, not held — it is currently doing work in P4/P5 planning and it may
be a metric artifact.

**What is NOT affected, stated so nobody redoes sound work:** the **C1 ladder's normalised returns**
(hz1x1 0→155, grid4x4 −1416→113, cologne3 −29→114) are computed from the logged **reward**, not from
ATT, and §5 defines tiers by measured normalised return. That axis stands. By that registered
definition MAPPO@1000 is above MaxPressure on all three; the open question is whether **ATT**, the
registered *reporting* metric, agrees — and a disagreement between reward-ranking and ATT-ranking would
itself be a finding worth the paper.

**Recomputation is arithmetic, not a re-collection** — `att_per_step[-1]` per episode, once
`datasets_v11/` exists. ⚠️ **One trap to not walk into:** the corpus is draws **1–200** while the
figures above are **nominal (draw 0)** from `run.py`. Recomputing over the corpus answers the
*randomised-draw* question and does **not** settle the nominal one; conflating them would repeat the
A2 error of quoting numbers from a different pipeline. Report them as the two distinct quantities D4
already says they are.

🚨 **AGGREGATION WARNING (2026-08-05): every number in the table above is the EPISODE-MEAN OF A RUNNING AVERAGE, not average travel time at the episode horizon.** `experiments/runner.py:168,175` samples `info["average_travel_time"]` each step and reports `_mean()` of those samples. The registered primary metric is the **horizon** value (`PREREGISTRATION.md` amendment A1, tag `v0.2-prereg-a1`). Measured 2026-08-05: MaxPressure on cf_hz1x1 is **160.56 episode-mean vs 247.75 horizon** (ratio 1.54); Random **307.5346 vs 413.53** (ratio 1.345) — the distortion is **policy-dependent**, so it moves effect sizes, not just levels. *(Corrected 2026-08-07: this line carried **317.46 vs 429.67**, the non-canonical ad-hoc-rollout figures that amendment **A2** retired on 2026-08-06. A2 fixed the pre-registration and nobody fixed §3.1, so retired numbers survived in the one section §7 makes the sanity anchor for every later phase. Found by running the bidirectional-propagation rule below once over the existing amendments — the first thing it was pointed at.)* **These anchors must be re-derived under A1 (task P8.0) before any reported result is compared against them.** Until then treat the table as *running-mean ATT*, a legacy harness statistic.

`output/experiments/p0_baselines/{results.json,summary.csv}`, which is **untracked and gitignored**, so

1. ⚠️ **CityFlow demand is deterministic on hz1x1 and grid4x4 ONLY** (MaxPressure σ=0 across engine seeds; **corrected 2026-08-06 — cf_cologne3 measures σ=3.32 over 5 seeds, mechanism unidentified; QUANTIFIED 2026-08-07 — it reaches bit-level trajectory divergence on 7.7 % of draws (3/39) under a fully deterministic MaxPressure policy, identical code, two runs one day apart, against a grid4x4 control of 0/6. Tier-level means are nevertheless stable to 0.080 %**) → seed-CIs capture policy stochasticity only; environment stochasticity must come from P6 perturbations / flow randomization. Affects P8 statistics design.
2. **MAPPO@60ep < random on grid4x4** → keep as motivating evidence (online MARL sample-inefficiency at 16 intersections); corroborated independently by thesis Fig. 5.4 (IPPO/MAPPO unconverged at 200 eps on hangzhou_4x4). Reproduce as a "performance vs. interaction budget" motivation figure. P2.1 convergence budget: ≥500 episodes on grid4x4, with explicit convergence verification.

## 4. Interface Contracts (FROZEN — paste into every sub-chat brief)

### 4.1 Environment API (non-standard Gym — do not "fix" it)
```python
info = env.reset(seed=42)                                (# returns info ONLY, no obs)
reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)   # reward FIRST
```
`action` is an `np.ndarray`, one action per intersection, **ordered by `[ix.id for ix in env.intersections]`**.

### 4.2 The `info` dict contract
```python
info = {
    "sim_time": float, "vehicle_count": int, "step": int,
    "average_travel_time": float,
    "lane_vehicle_count": {lane_id: int}, "lane_waiting_vehicle_count": {lane_id: int},
    "metrics": {metric_name: float},                # requested global metrics
    "intersections": {
        ix_id: {
            "state": [...],                          # per-intersection obs vector
            "avail_actions": [0, 2],                 # legal actions NOW (masking!)
            "current_phase": int, "time_in_phase": int,
            "action_applied": bool,
            "metrics": {name: float},
            "reward": float,                         # ONLY if local_reward_fn set
        }, ...
    },
}
```

### 4.3 Agent API (mirror `agent/MAPPOAgent.py`)
```python
class MyAgent(BaseAgent):
    def __init__(self, gym_env, ..., device=None, seed=None)   # reads env.intersections
    def act(self, info, explore=True, update_memory=True) -> np.ndarray
    def observe(self, next_info, reward, terminated, truncated=False) -> dict
    def save(self, path); def load(self, path)
```
Helper functions in `agent/utils/utils.py` (USE THESE, do not reimplement):
`Utils.infer_action_counts(env.action_space, intersections)`, `Utils.extract_per_intersection_info(info, ids)`, `Utils.state_from_info(ix_payload)`, `Utils.extract_valid_actions(ix_payload, n_actions)`, `Utils.scalar_reward(reward)`, `Utils.reward_for_intersection(...)`, `Utils.resolve_device(device)`, `Utils.seed_everything(seed)`.

### 4.4 Coding standards
Python ≥3.12, PyTorch, type hints (`from __future__ import annotations`), numpy `float32` for stored arrays, docstrings in the repo's style, no new heavy deps without a Decisions-Log entry, `pytest` tests for everything, no modifications to `envs/base_traffic_env.py` unless a task explicitly says so.

---

## 5. Offline Data Design (agreed v0.1 — refine in P1/P3)

- **Format:** one `.npz` (compressed) per episode + one `manifest.json` per collection run.
- **Per step, per intersection:** `state (float32)`, `action (int64)`, `avail_actions mask (bool, width = n_actions)`, `local_reward (float32, NaN if unset)`, `current_phase`, `time_in_phase`.
- **Per step, global:** `global_reward`, `vehicle_count`, `sim_time`, selected `metrics`.
- **Episode metadata:** scenario id, backend, engine seed, `delta_time`, `max_steps`, `state_features` list, reward fn names, phase-control mode, behavior-policy id + checkpoint hash, repo git hash, timestamp.
- **Dataset ladder (C1's experimental axis; released publicly as a contribution, positioned vs DTRL):** tiers are defined by **measured normalized return** (random = 0, MaxPressure = 100, computed per scenario post-collection), NOT by policy names — our own P0.2 data shows MaxPressure > MAPPO@60ep, so policy names imply a false quality ordering (critique 2026-07-11b). Source policies (= dataset provenance metadata): random, fixed-time, MaxPressure, MAPPO checkpoints (60/200/converged), ε-noised MAPPO (ε ∈ {0.1, 0.3}), DQN(CNN), mixed. The paper's ladder table reports the measured return distribution of each dataset. If no learned policy beats MaxPressure after convergence, the top slice IS MaxPressure — which sharpens, not weakens, the "does MADT exceed its data" question.
- **Demand randomization is mandatory for every collected episode (P2.0):** with deterministic CityFlow flows, N episodes of a deterministic policy = 1 trajectory × N; effective corpus size of deterministic tiers ≈ 0 (critique 2026-07-11a). Each episode = (scenario, flow-draw seed); nominal flow = draw 0. Randomizer operates at the vehicle-list level (verified: flow.json = per-vehicle insertions with routes) so draws render to BOTH backends (flow.json ↔ .rou.xml) — preserves C3's paired scenarios and fixes P8's environment-stochasticity problem at the source. Logger convention: flow-draw id goes into run_metadata (no logger code change needed).

## 6. Task Checklist

> **The checklist IS the state. Answering the question directly, because it was asked after
> reading it and believing it (2026-08-07):** §8 is *why* a thing was decided, §10 is *what is in
> flight today*, and **§6 is what is done and what is not.** A checkbox is inherently a state
> claim — there is no way to write `[ ]` that means "look elsewhere" — so it is maintained, not
> annotated with a disclaimer. **A stale checklist is a false claim in the most-read part of this
> document.**
>
> **Binding rule: the merge commit that lands a task ticks its boxes IN THE SAME COMMIT.** A
> follow-up step is a step that gets skipped; that is exactly how P3.1–P3.4 sat unticked while
> `offline/dataset.py` was merged, reviewed and on `main`.
>
> **Mechanical drift check — every Return Packet on `main` must have a ticked box:**
> ```bash
> for f in $(git ls-tree --name-only main docs/returns/ | grep -v TEMPLATE); do
>   id=$(basename "$f" .md); base=${id%%-*}
>   git show main:docs/PROJECT_PLAN.md | grep -qE "^- \[x\] \*\*${base}" \
>     || echo "DRIFT: $id has a Return Packet and no ticked box"
> done
> ```
> It found three violations on 2026-08-07 (P2.2-draws, P2.6, P3) and is the check to run before
> trusting this section. Automating it as a test is `DEFERRED.md` row 26.
>
> ⚠️ **WHAT THIS CHECK CANNOT SEE, so nobody reads a green invariant as a verified plan.** It
> catches *"merged but unticked"*. It cannot catch **"ticked but the artifact does not do what the
> item says"** — which is the **P0.3 mis-tick**, ticked from a commit message that actually
> recorded a *failure*, and it survived **weeks**. That failure mode is unreachable by any
> structural check, because the box and the Return Packet both exist and both look right; only
> reading the code catches it. **A green invariant means the bookkeeping agrees, never that the
> work is what it claims to be.**

We concluded "never a deadlock, just oversubscription; not reproducible; cause unknown, most likely environmental." **That was wrong.** The P0.3-fix reviewer produced real hangs on demand (`exit=124`, killed at 120 s and 150 s) and isolated the mechanism: `fork()` + libgomp — a child entering an OpenMP parallel region with `nthreads > 1` waits forever on team threads `fork()` never duplicated. Our own benchmark's `workers=6 unpinned ≥1200 s / cap` was almost certainly that hang, which we recorded as "slow." The July report was right about the symptom; our refutation over-reached from "we did not reproduce it in 20 min" to "it does not happen." What WAS correctly refuted: the spawn patch as the fix, and all four stated mechanisms. Real fix = P0.3-fix
- [x] **P0.3-fix** ✅ DONE 2026-08-03, merged (`64800fb`, one +45-line authorised delta in `experiments/runner.py`; 249 tests). **THE PIN IS A LIVENESS FIX, NOT A PERFORMANCE FIX** — the reviewer's inert-mutant experiment shows the fork+libgomp hang IS reachable against unpinned code (`exit=124` at 120 s / 150 s) and unreachable against the committed code (14 passed, 10/10 runs). `torch.set_num_threads(1)` before the first parallel region makes the child run serially in the master thread, so there is nothing to wait on. Speed (199.2 s → 50.2 s, ≈3.97× at workers=6, measured in one session from a clean shell) is a **side effect**. Evidence: `docs/reviews/P0.3-fix.md`, `docs/returns/P0.3-fix.md`

Nothing on disk says the pin prevents an unbounded hang; docstring, plan and packet all justified it on speed alone. A maintainer could reasonably make it conditional on `workers > 1` and silently reintroduce a hang with no failure message (`run_matrix` uses `as_completed` + `future.result()` with no timeout). **Needs a NEW frozen-file authorisation — the P0.3-fix authorisation covered only "limiting per-worker torch thread counts" and is spent**
- [x] **P0.6** ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04** (merged `e01b0d1`). `experiments/configs/p0_threading_bench.json` committed and run: **182.9 s → 48.3 s, ≈3.79× at workers=6**, measured in one session from a clean shell. **Deliberately NOT reconciled** with the recorded 199.2/50.2 — different machine state is expected, and quietly adopting the older pair would recreate the cross-session error the ban exists for. §3.1 anchor data now tracked at `docs/data/p0_baselines/` with `docs/p0_baseline_numbers.md` and a double-compute test that passed first run. Cost is recurring and will be paid on every future guard change. **Design ruled 2026-08-04:** treat a single character delimited by backticks as a code literal and blank it before matching (same mechanism as `ALLOWED_NAMES`), rather than removing o-acute from the class (it is common in genuine Polish prose) or excluding the guard wholesale (that would stop checking a real file). `check_english.sh` is a guard-level `FROZEN_EXCEPTIONS` entry but is **denied at permission level**, so this ships as a patch under `docs/patches/`

P4.4 filled **one column of twenty**; C1 claims a data-quality ladder and currently has comparators at a single rung. ~~`evaluate_arm` already takes arbitrary arms, so this is a config loop~~ 🚨 **CORRECTED 2026-08-13 by reading `offline/offline_baselines.py`, and the false claim was MINE: that is HALF TRUE and the wrong half is the expensive one.** Training and evaluation *are* a config loop (`--dataset-dir`). **The REPORTING path is not:** `_run_report` **hard-requires the `mappo1000` arm** (`:2367`), defines `recovered_fraction` against it by name (`:2376`, `:2410-2413`), and emits an equivalence **verdict unconditionally** (`:2383-2428`) — which this very item's own "NO equivalence verdicts" ruling forbids, so the ruling was **not satisfiable by configuration** at the moment it was written. A true statement about one function, written as a description of the whole pipeline: the signature error, in the plan, by the coordinator. Generalisation is required and is regression-gated on `docs/data/p4_4_baselines.json` regenerating byte-identically. See `BRIEF_17` §11.A2. Cost estimate stands: measured costs give ≈1.2 h training + ≈35 min evaluation **per tier**, so ≈9 h total, no new corpus **collection**. ⚠️ **Two design constraints, both registered here before it runs.** **(a) OUR LADDER CONFOUNDS QUALITY WITH STATE COVERAGE** — `mappo1000` is high-quality and narrow, `random` is low-quality and broad — whereas a mixture corpus varies quality at roughly constant coverage. A ranking that moves along our ladder therefore cannot be attributed to *quality* alone, and no claim may do so. **(b) The fix is nearly free and we already own the data:** mixture tiers (e.g. 25/50/75 % expert) are a **resampling of the corpus we have collected**, not a new campaign, so the grid can carry both axes — policy-identity *and* expert-fraction — without a new collection campaign *(phrase "for one config loop" retired 2026-08-13 with the correction above; the data construction is free, the reporting path is not)*. The second axis is what makes us directly comparable to OffLight's design. **Gate:** per-tier δ must be re-derived (A6's δ is `mappo1000`-specific) and the derivation declared before any number. **Sequencing: after P4.3**, because the DT arm must use the calibrated prompt or the grid measures the un-calibrated DT five times instead of once. ⭐ **THE GRID'S REAL TARGET, registered 2026-08-12 (the user's framing, sharpened): %BC is THREE DIFFERENT MECHANISMS under one method name, and the grid should be built to separate them, not to draw a performance curve.** `pure expert → the filter selects the best CHECKPOINTS` (**measured**: F3, `{202:10, 101:9, 505:1, 303:0, 404:0}`, r = −0.991); `mixture → it selects the EXPERT FRACTION` (classic filtered-BC, and the only regime OffLight's data speaks to); `pure random → it selects the EASIEST DEMAND DRAWS and the luckiest rollouts`, which should not transfer. **The distinguishing feature is not quality-versus-coverage but that a mixture always contains some expert fraction while `random` contains none.** ✅ **And the instrument to separate them already exists and costs no training:** the per-tier version of the two checks that settled F3 — arrival volume of the kept episodes against the rest, and overlap of the kept set with MaxPressure's own difficulty ranking, MaxPressure being deterministic and therefore a policy-independent difficulty probe. **Report, for every tier, WHAT the top-decile filter selected.** Three mechanisms named and measured under one method label is a stronger result than a curve, and it is not available to anyone who reports only the curve.

- [x] **P4.7** ✅ **DONE 2026-08-15 — THE COMPOSITION AXIS FALSIFIED A READING TAKEN FROM THE LADDER THAT CONFOUNDS QUALITY WITH COVERAGE, WHICH IS WHAT IT WAS BUILT FOR.** Merged after `docs/reviews/P4.7.md` (**PASS-WITH-NOTES**, 2 MAJOR, no blockers) and the fix round `BRIEF_20`: **0 numeric leaves changed**, suite **849 passed, 3 skipped**. **Result:** `bc_top10` leads all three mixtures (**104.34 / 104.24 / 103.24**), `dt` is 2/4 on all three — **its best standing anywhere AS OF 2026-08-15; superseded 2026-08-18, when P5.1's `dt_nomix` took 1 of 5 on grid4x4** — and still **0 of 8 tiers led, all eight on `cf_hz1x1`** — and **IQL inverts, 1/4 on the weak single-controller tiers against 3/4 on all three mixtures**. **§1b's R3 is falsified and replaced by a mechanism** (*"how weak AND how unimodal"*), surviving **three independent operationalisations**, two of which use no constructed quantity. ⚠️ **Three defects worth carrying:** **Q1 was UNFALSIFIABLE BY CONSTRUCTION** — the components' returns are disjoint by ~29,600 units, **the coordinator's design error** (`DEFERRED` 46 records the fix: `mappo1000+mappo200` at 43.5 % overlap); **Q2's "decisively" was withdrawn** because `mix33 > mix50` reverses on **2 of 5 training seeds** (t = 1.14) while its other conjuncts are robust, and the companion CIs average the seeds away; and **a pre-registered disclosure was not delivered while a docstring claimed it was** (39/48/44 draws in both components, now emitted with the registered **weakened** label). **Q2 HELD and is the task's only falsifiable-and-confirmed registered prediction.** Evidence: `output/p4_7/` (60 checkpoints, 15 cells, **112/112** verified both directions). Original item: ⭐ **THE MIXTURE TIERS — P4.6's phase 2, separated on 2026-08-14 exactly as `BRIEF_17` §3 registered it would be if phase 1 stood alone.** Three OffLight-matched compositions — **33 / 50 / 67 % expert + random** — × four methods = **12 cells, ≈5 h**, **built by RESAMPLING episodes we already own**; no new collection and no new rollout protocol. **Why it is not optional:** §1's constraint (a) records that our single-controller ladder **confounds data quality with state coverage**, and a mixture varies composition at roughly constant coverage — **so this is the design that separates the two axes**, and no P4.6 ranking may be attributed to quality alone until it runs. It is also **the only regime OffLight's data speaks to**, making the composition axis directly comparable to the closest published work. ⚠️ **Inherits, and the brief must say so explicitly:** `BRIEF_17` **§11.A1** (size-match by taking one episode per draw, never a uniform subsample), **§11.A5** (report what the top-decile filter selected on **BOTH** axes — expert-vs-random **and** a seed histogram — or "it selects the expert fraction" is confounded with the checkpoint selection P4.5 already measured), **§4** (no equivalence verdicts; paired difference, CI, CI width and rank-biserial), and **§12** (the campaign runs in a user-started `tmux` session). ⭐ **It is where R2's open question becomes answerable:** P4.6's §1b records that the filter *changes sign* on weak single-controller tiers, but **P3 — the diagnostic for "it selects noise" — FAILED on `random`**, so *what* it selects there is unknown. A mixture is the one corpus where the thing it should select (the expert fraction) is known by construction.
